*Haowei Liu,Runze He,Jian Lu,Ao Ma,Run Ling,Ke Cao,Jiasong Feng,Wei Feng,Shuo Lu,Yexing Xu,Yun Wang,Jing Wang,Zhanjie Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Unifies diffusion-based visual text generation and editing in a single DiT model called InnoText, introducing font-size aware modulation, small-character augmentation, and region-weighted loss, aided by a bilingual English-Chinese dataset.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Visual text tasks require precise structure and legibility, especially for small text and non-Latin scripts; existing UNet-based approaches struggle with text clarity and DiT-based methods are often single-task limited, hindering generalization and workflow efficiency.

Method: Present InnoText, a unified DiT-based framework featuring Font Size-Aware Modulation (FSAM), Small-Character Aware Augmentation, and a Task-Specific Region Weighted Loss; includes creation of a high-quality bilingual English-Chinese visual text dataset for training and evaluation.

Result: Demonstrates superior generation accuracy and editing quality, yielding visually appealing and realistic text images.

Conclusion: A unified, task-general DiT-based approach with FSAM, targeted augmentations, and region-weighted optimization enhances text fidelity and cross-task generalization in visual text generation/editing, supported by a bilingual dataset.

Abstract: Diffusion models have recently achieved remarkable success in high-fidelity image synthesis, yet their application to visual text generation and editing remains relatively underexplored. Unlike general image generation, visual text tasks demand precise structural regularity and legibility, which may pose additional challenges for small-scale text and non-Latin scripts such as Chinese. Existing UNet-based models often struggle to produce clear and coherent text, while DiT-based models, though more expressive, are typically limited to a single task, which may lead to redundant training pipelines, inconsistent visual styles, and reduced cross-task generalization. To address these challenges, we propose InnoText, a unified DiT-based framework capable of performing both text generation and editing within a single model. We introduce a Font Size-Aware Modulation (FSAM) module to enhance representations across font scales, a Small-Character Aware Augmentation strategy to improve fine-grained fidelity, and a Task-Specific Region Weighted Loss for adaptive optimization. To support training and evaluation, we also construct a high-quality bilingual (English-Chinese) visual text dataset covering diverse fonts, sizes, and backgrounds. Experimental results demonstrate that our method achieves superior generation accuracy and editing quality, producing visually appealing and realistic text images.

</details>


### [27] [Projection Pursuit CPCANet for Domain Generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22117)
*Yu-Hsi Chen,Abd-Krim Seghouane*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: PP-CPCANet is a covariance-free domain generalization method that learns a global orthogonal basis on the Stiefel manifold via the Cayley transform, addressing CPCANet's rank deficiency and achieving state-of-the-art performance with stable training.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: CPCANet's batch-wise CPCA suffers from rank-deficient covariance estimation due to small mini-batch sizes, hindering reliable extraction of common principal components (CPCs).

Method: Proposes Projection Pursuit CPCANet (PP-CPCANet): a covariance-free framework that learns a global orthogonal basis on the Stiefel manifold and optimizes it jointly with network parameters via the Cayley transform; introduces a symmetry-breaking detached-median PP dispersion objective to provide dense optimization signals for CPCs.

Result: Experimental evaluation on four domain generalization benchmarks shows PP-CPCANet achieves state-of-the-art performance while maintaining stable training.

Conclusion: PP-CPCANet mitigates rank-deficiency in CPCANet by learning a global orthogonal basis in a covariance-free manner, enabling robust CPC extraction and yielding superior DG performance with stable training.

Abstract: Domain Generalization (DG) aims to learn representations robust to distribution shifts. Recent geometric alignment methods, such as CPCANet, extract domain-invariant structures through batch-wise Common Principal Component Analysis (CPCA). However, CPCANet suffers from rank-deficient covariance estimation due to the small-sample-size issue in mini-batch training. To address this limitation, we propose Projection Pursuit CPCANet (PP-CPCANet), a covariance-free framework that learns a global orthogonal basis on the Stiefel manifold and jointly optimizes it with network parameters via the Cayley transform. We further introduce a symmetry-breaking detached-median PP dispersion objective to extract common principal components (CPCs) with dense and robust optimization signals. Experiments on four DG benchmarks show that PP-CPCANet achieves SOTA performance while maintaining stable training.

</details>


### [28] [JustDepth: Real-Time Radar-Camera Depth Estimation with Single-Scan LiDAR Supervision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22172)
*Wooyung Yun,Dongwook Kim,Soomok Lee*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: JustDepth is a single-stage radar-camera depth estimator that achieves accurate depth with dramatically lower latency and reduced stripe artifacts by aggregating radar into a fixed-width representation, fusing features with a Height Fusion Block, propagating depth via a lightweight GNN, and using a training-only confidence decoder, trained with radar, camera, and a single-scan LiDAR.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Cameras provide rich appearance but lack metric depth; radar provides metric range but is sparse and noisy. Existing pipelines are multi-stage or rely on auxiliary annotations, causing higher latency and limited portability. A single-stage, annotation-light, robust method is desirable.

Method: Key components: 1) fixed-width 1D radar representation to decouple runtime from point count; 2) Height Fusion Block to fuse modalities; 3) lightweight GNN to propagate depth globally; 4) training-only confidence decoder to stabilize learning with zero test-time cost; 5) simple augmentations to mitigate stripe artifacts; VHGR to quantify artifacts.

Result: On nuScenes, JustDepth achieves comparable accuracy to recent SOTA while reducing inference time by 39.7x and reducing stripe artifacts by 66% as measured by VHGR.

Conclusion: Single-stage radar-camera depth estimation using radar, camera, and limited LiDAR can deliver accurate, low-latency depth with fewer artifacts, improving portability and real-time applicability.

Abstract: Accurate yet low-latency depth is essential for radar-camera perception in autonomous systems. Cameras provide rich appearance but lack metric scale, whereas automotive radar offers metric range but is sparse and noisy. Many pipelines are multi-stage or depend on auxiliary annotations, increasing latency and limiting portability. We introduce JustDepth, a single-stage radar-camera depth estimator trained only with radar, camera, and single-scan LiDAR. All radar returns are aggregated into a fixed-width 1D representation, decoupling runtime from point count. A Height Fusion Block fuses modalities, a lightweight GNN propagates depth globally, and a training-only confidence decoder stabilizes learning with zero test-time cost. We mitigate stripe artifacts via simple augmentations and quantify them using the Vertical-Horizontal Gradient Ratio (VHGR). On nuScenes, compared to recent state-of-the-art methods, JustDepth maintains accuracy while reducing inference time by 39.7x and stripe artifacts by 66% as measured by VHGR.

</details>


### [29] [A Framework for Individual Tree Growth Reconstruction Using Multi-Platform Laser Scanning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22129)
*Daniella Tavi,Valtteri Soininen,Lassi Ruoppa,Jesse Muhojoki,Juha Hyyppä*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Multitemporal cross-platform Lidar framework for estimating individual-tree DBH and stem-volume growth in boreal forests using MLS/TLS stem curves and ALS heights, with deep-learning delineation and height-growth scaling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Overcome scarce historical stem data and sensor-propagation errors to enable reliable tree delineation and cross-temporal correspondence for growth estimation.

Method: Delineate trees from MLS with deep learning; transfer segmentation to ALS/TLS clouds; derive stem curves from MLS/TLS; estimate heights from ALS; apply a height-growth-based scaling model to reconstruct time-series stem attributes; compare modeled growth to manual estimates.

Result: Modeled growth aligns better with manual growth than differencing independently estimated attributes. 5- and 10-year growth RMSEs: DBH 55–111% and 26–67%; volume 31–87% and 21–67%, varying by plot difficulty. Scaling model is temporally robust; errors stabilize after 5–6 years; maximum RMSEs after 12 years: 8–12% for DBH and 12–23% for volume.

Conclusion: Combining MLS/TLS stem measurements with multitemporal ALS heights provides a robust framework for individual-tree growth estimation without requiring multiple under-canopy scans.

Abstract: Accurate tree-level forest monitoring using laser scanning data requires reliable tree delineation, consistent tree correspondence across multitemporal point clouds, and accurate estimation of tree attributes and their change. Reconstructing tree growth in boreal forests is challenging due to the scarcity of historical stem-level data, propagation of errors from older sensors into change estimation, and growth rates with a magnitude of measurement uncertainty. This study investigates a framework for estimating individual tree diameter at breast height (DBH) and stem volume growth using 136 point clouds acquired between 2014--2025 with 11 scanners on airborne (ALS), mobile (MLS), and terrestrial laser scanning (TLS) platforms across boreal forest test sites. Trees were delineated from an MLS point cloud using deep learning-based segmentation which was transferred to the remaining point clouds, resulting in reliable multitemporal tree correspondence. Stem curves were derived from MLS/TLS data, with ALS data used for height estimation, enabling DBH and volume estimation and time series. A height growth-based scaling model was used to reconstruct stem attributes across time and estimate growth. Results showed that modeled growth achieved higher agreement with manual growth estimates than differencing independently estimated attributes from point clouds. The modeled-manual 5- and 10-year growth RMSEs were 55--111\% and 26--67\% for DBH, and 31--87\% and 21--67\% for volume, respectively, depending on plot difficulty. The scaling model was temporally robust, with errors remaining stable or stabilizing after 5--6 years, reaching maximum RMSEs of 8--12\% for DBH and 12--23\% for volume after 12 years. Combining MLS/TLS-derived stem measurements with multitemporal ALS-derived heights provided a robust framework for individual tree growth estimation without requiring multiple under-canopy scans.

</details>


### [30] [Geometric 2D Scene Graph Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22325)
*Christoph Jahn,Urs Waldmann,Bastian Goldluecke*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A data-efficient pipeline to construct scene graphs for assembly relations without semantic data, using Faster R-CNN outputs, a transformer to produce an adjacency matrix, and a Siamese-aGCN for relation characterization, validated on toy components.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Need to reason about how parts fit together in production and robotics, especially with small datasets and without semantic labels to guide assembly understanding.

Method: Geometric representations from Faster R-CNN outputs are transformed by a transformer into an adjacency matrix; this feeds a Siamese network that uses message passing via an attentional graph convolutional network (aGCN) to characterize component connections.

Result: Demonstrates feasibility on a study dataset of toy model components that can assemble into transportation vehicles; the method constructs scene graphs capturing assembly relationships without relying on semantics.

Conclusion: The approach offers a data-efficient way to infer assembly relations and could support planning/execution in robotic assembly tasks without heavy semantic annotation.

Abstract: In production processes for consumer products, assembly instructions are essential not only for planning but also for executing the production process. Likewise in robotics, it is crucial for an assembly robot to understand how components fit together and can be assembled. To facilitate these tasks, we contribute a method for constructing scene graphs to represent and characterize assembly relationships between components. Our approach does not rely on semantic data and is capable of handling a very small dataset. To realize this, the output of a Faster R-CNN model is used to create geometric representations, which are then processed by a transformer architecture to generate an adjacency matrix. This matrix serves as input to a Siamese network that uses message passing based on an attentional graph convolutional network (aGCN) architecture to characterize the connections between the components. We validate our method on a study dataset of toy model components which can be assembled into transportation vehicles.

</details>


### [31] [GLI-AL: A Multi-Modal Glioma MRI Label Resource with Unified Anatomy-Lesion Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22135)
*Xingyu Xiang,Shuang Hao,Fan Wang,Jianhua Ma,Chunfeng Lian*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces BraTS-GLI Anatomy-Lesion, a labels-only resource that expands BraTS-GLI with eight anatomy-lesion classes, enabling WMH-aware joint segmentation and robust evaluation; includes repaired-image inputs and metadata, with a purified and extended cohort.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing BraTS-GLI annotations focus on tumor subregions and neglect coexisting white matter hyperintensities (WMH), causing label noise in joint anatomy-lesion segmentation. A unified, labeled resource with WMH-aware supervision is needed for robust training and evaluation.

Method: Derive eight-class anatomy-lesion labels from the BraTS-2023-GLI training cohort as a labels-only resource (1,251 label sets) aligned to the four-modal MRI cases, including image-repair labels for 116 cases. Organize into a purified 394-case subset and an extended 857-case subset. Provide case-level metadata (label source, image-repair requirements, QC status, access, checksums, release boundaries) and align with the original annotations to enable joint supervision and label-noise analysis.

Result: Validation study using MedNeXt and T1/FLAIR inputs shows WMH-aware supervision preserves healthy-tissue segmentation performance in-domain (GLI) and external WMH datasets, while improving sensitivity to coexisting lesions compared with noisy-control training.

Conclusion: The resource supports scientific research for joint anatomy-lesion supervision, label-noise analysis, and reproducible evaluation. Data and code are publicly available, enabling WMH-aware segmentation research and robust evaluation across datasets.

Abstract: Existing BraTS-GLI datasets provide a widely used benchmark for adult glioma MRI segmentation, but their task definition focuses on tumor subregions and does not systematically represent coexisting white matter hyperintensities (WMH). In joint segmentation settings, such unlabeled abnormalities introduce task-specific label noise by treating pathological regions as normal tissue. To address this limitation, we introduce BraTS-GLI Anatomy-Lesion, a controlled-access, labels-only derived resource built from the BraTS 2023-GLI training cohort. The resource provides 1,251 unified eight-class anatomy-lesion label sets aligned with the original four-modal MRI cases, including image-repair labels for 116 cases requiring repaired imaging inputs. The cohort is organized into a 394-case purified subset and an 857-case extended subset, with case-level metadata covering label source, image-repair requirements, quality-control status, access conditions, checksums, and release boundaries. Compared with the original BraTS-GLI annotations, the resource substantially expands foreground supervision by incorporating healthy brain tissues and previously unlabeled coexisting abnormalities within a unified label space. A validation study using MedNeXt and T1/FLAIR inputs suggests that WMH-aware supervision preserves healthy-tissue segmentation performance across both in-domain GLI and external WMH datasets, while improving sensitivity to coexisting lesions relative to noisy-control training. The resource is intended for scientific research and supports joint anatomy-lesion supervision, label-noise analysis, and reproducible evaluation. Data are available at https://www.synapse.org/Synapse:syn75210889/wiki/, and code is available at https://github.com/xyx200/brats-gli-anatomy-lesion-code. The data resource DOI is https://doi.org/10.7303/SYN75210889.

</details>


### [32] [SM4RT: Learning Structured Motion Geometry for 4D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22534)
*Shing Ho J. Lin,Wenzhao Zheng,Dong Zhuo,Yuqi Wu,Jie Zhou,Jiwen Lu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces SM4RT, a transformer-based framework for 4D monocular reconstruction that enforces rigid-body structure by modeling motion as a small set of SE(3) twists (motion bases) with per-pixel, time-shared weights, enabling end-to-end 3D geometry and motion estimation from monocular video.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing motion methods treat motion as unstructured per-pixel displacements, failing to capture rigid-body coherence; 4D dynamic reconstruction from monocular RGB videos is underconstrained; there's a need to integrate geometric structure into motion perception.

Method: Proposes Structure-of-Motion decomposition into a compact set of motion bases, each a temporal sequence of 6D twists in SE(3). Dense motion is recovered via sparse, time-shared per-pixel assignment weights over these bases. Introduces parallel motion geometry encoder and decoder to jointly infer 3D geometry, world-coordinate motion, and scene kinematic structure in a single forward pass from monocular RGB video.

Result: Claims strong motion reconstruction performance while preserving the geometric structure of scene motion.

Conclusion: By representing scene dynamics with a compact SE(3) motion-basis structure and learning geometry and motion jointly, SM4RT enables coherent 4D reconstructions from monocular video and respects rigid-body kinematics, suggesting improved temporal consistency and physical plausibility.

Abstract: Geometry Foundation Models (GFMs) have substantially advanced monocular 3D reconstruction, yet extending this capability to 4D dynamic understanding remains a fundamental challenge. Most existing motion perception methods (e.g., sparse tracking, dense point-wise flow) treat motion as independent point-wise displacements, ignoring the structured nature of physical motion. However, real-world objects usually obey rigid-body kinematics, and points thus usually move collectively, not in isolation. Motion itself possesses geometric structure: physical objects undergo a set of rigid-body transformations governed by SE(3), rather than unstructured point-wise displacements. Building on this insight, we propose SM4RT, a Structured Motion 4D Reconstruction Transformer for end-to-end 3D reconstruction and structured motion perception. SM4RT introduces Structure-of-Motion to represent scene dynamics, where scene motion is decomposed into a compact set of motion bases, each represented as a temporal sequence of 6D twists in SE(3). Dense scene motion is then recovered by sparse, time-shared per-pixel assignment weights over these bases, ensuring points on the same object share a common rigid-body motion trajectory. SM4RT introduces a parallel motion geometry encoder and decoder that jointly infer 3D geometry, world-coordinate motion, and scene kinematic structure in a single forward pass from monocular RGB video. SM4RT achieves strong motion reconstruction performance while preserving the geometric structure of scene motion.

</details>


### [33] [CARDIAG: A Dense Segment Classification Benchmark of Deep Learning Architectures for Coronary Angiography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22139)
*Dominik Bernard Lau,Hubert Malinowski,Jerzy Szyjut,Adam Brzeski,Tomasz Dziubich,Radosław Targoński,Tomasz Figatowski,Natalia Zielińska*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A new benchmark (CARDIAG) for pixel-level SYNTAX segmentation in coronary angiograms across 24 architectures. ConvNeXt V2 + DeepLab V3 Plus topped the solo results (macro F1=0.456) and improved to 0.479 with ensembling (Mamba U-Net + FPN). The dataset is multi-center with rich labels (SYNTAX, uncertainty, masks, frames, DICOM metadata) and a careful split accounting for diameter error, overlap, centerline quality, and calibration. The study analyzes calibration, generalization of top methods, and data efficiency, highlighting the value of both high- and low-resolution features and model correctness across demographics, vessel sides, and projection angles. The benchmark aims to standardize evaluation for SYNTAX segmentation and related tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a standardized, robust benchmark for pixel-level coronary angiogram analysis, enabling fair comparison of diverse deep learning architectures and ensuring calibration, data efficiency, and generalization across multi-center data.

Method: Evaluate 24 architectures from classic ConvNets to vision-language/space-based algorithms on the CARDIAG dataset with a careful split. Use metrics that include diameter error, overlap, centerline quality, and calibration. Report macro F1 for SYNTAX segmentation, perform ensembling (ConvNeXt V2 + DeepLab V3 Plus, then with Mamba U-Net and FPN). Assess calibration, generalization of top models, data efficiency, and the role of multi-scale features. Examine correctness with respect to patient demographics, vessel sides, and projection angles.

Result: Best single model: ConvNeXt V2 encoder + DeepLab V3 Plus decoder, macro F1=0.456. Ensembling with Mamba U-Net and Feature Pyramid Network improves macro F1 to 0.479. The architectures show good calibration. Top-5 methods’ generalization and data efficiency analyzed. Highlights the importance of both high- and low-resolution features for encoding; demographic/vessel/projection configurations tested for model correctness.

Conclusion: The CARDIAG benchmark enables rigorous, standardized evaluation for SYNTAX segmentation and related tasks like lesion detection. It provides a multi-center, richly annotated dataset with careful evaluation protocols, supporting robust comparisons and future methodological advances.

Abstract: Accurate pixel-level classification of coronary angiograms is critical for cardiovascular disease assessment, yet the field lacks standardized evaluation protocols. In this work we demonstrate a new benchmark for the assessment of deep learning models which densely classify pixels of coronary angiograms to one of SYNTAX classes (or background). The evaluation covers 24 distinct architectures starting with classic convnets to recent state-space-based vision algorithms. We release CARDIAG - a multi-center, multi-label dataset which we carefully split to reliably compute metrics, accounting for diameter error, overlap, centerline quality and calibration. The data contains SYNTAX labels, binary, uncertainty and segmentation masks as well as intermediate frames together with the selected non-sensitive DICOM metadata. From the multitude of algorithms, we nominate ConvNeXt V2 encoder with DeepLab V3 Plus decoder as the best performing, achieving macro $F_1=0.456$, which we then ensemble with Mamba U-Net and Feature Pyramid Network, for an increased $F_1=0.479$. We demonstrate all the architectures to be well calibrated and determine the generalization of the top 5 methods, together with the data efficiency of these architectures. We highlight the importance of both high-resolution and low-resolution features in encoding. We also demonstrate the model correctness in the context of patient demographic, vessel sides and projection angle configurations. Overall the released benchmark allows for future studies to robustly and rigorously assess the proposals, not only for SYNTAX segmentation, but lesion detection and many more.

</details>


### [34] [Visual Relocalization from Sparse Views in Aliased and Low-Texture Environments via Novel View Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22147)
*Maria Peribañez,Javier Civera,Rudolph Triebel,Riccardo Giubilato*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A geometry-aware visual relocalization method using 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) with multimodal depth supervision (MVS + LiDAR) to improve single-image 6-DoF pose estimation in low-texture, harsh-illumination planetary-like terrains; shows robustness and accuracy gains and provides open-source code.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Planetary-like terrains exhibit severe localization challenges due to low texture, perceptual aliasing, harsh illumination, and sparse, weakly overlapping viewpoints, causing traditional image-to-image and image-to-map methods to fail.

Method: Constructs a differentiable 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) map and directly regresses camera pose against it. Introduces a geometry-aware training strategy that fuses photometric losses with geometric supervision derived from multi-view stereo (MVS) and LiDAR depths to better fit the scene geometry.

Result: Experiments on planetary-analog data show substantial relocalization accuracy improvements, with enhanced photometric and geometric consistency and more robust single-image 6-DoF pose estimation; code is released.

Conclusion: Joint photometric-geometric optimization against a differentiable 3DGS map, guided by multimodal depth supervision (MVS+LiDAR), yields improved geometry fidelity and relocalization robustness in challenging terrain contexts.

Abstract: Visual localization becomes extremely challenging in planetary-like terrains characterized by low texture, perceptual aliasing, harsh illumination, and sparse, weakly overlapping viewpoints induced by forward rover motion and unconstrained driving directions. Under these conditions, state-of-the-art image-to-image and image-to-map matching pipelines suffer significant performance degradation. In this work, we propose a visual relocalization method that departs from classical correspondence-based pipelines by directly estimating camera poses against a differentiable map representation built with 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS). Our key contribution is a geometry-aware training strategy that combines photometric and geometric losses, where the geometric supervision is provided for the first time by combining multi-view stereo (MVS) and LiDAR depths. We show that this joint optimization produces a 3DGS model that better fits the underlying scene geometry, leading to improved photometric and geometric consistency and more robust, accurate single-image 6-DoF pose estimation. Extensive experiments on data acquired in planetary-analog environments validate the effectiveness of our approach, showing substantial gains in relocalization accuracy under challenging conditions. Code is available at https://github.com/DLR-RM/multimodal-gsplat-relocalization.

</details>


### [35] [dRAE: Representation Autoencoder with Hyper-Spherical Codes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22148)
*Tianren Ma,Lin Long,Chuyan Chen,Mu Zhang,Junbo Zhao,Tong Zhang,Qixiang Ye*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Hyper-Spherical Quantization (HSQ) introduces angular routing to discretize high-dimensional visual representations, decoupling semantic content from magnitude to overcome codebook collapse and scale-dominated quantization. It enables a discrete Representation Autoencoder (dRAE) with scalable codebooks up to 131k entries and 100% utilization, improving reconstruction fidelity and semantic coherence for integration with language models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Quantizing high-dimensional visual features for language-model alignment is plagued by codebook collapse and scale-sensitive assignments due to Euclidean objectives in anisotropic feature spaces, hindering scalability and semantic preservation.

Method: Hyper-Spherical Quantization (HSQ) that uses angular routing to separate semantic content from feature magnitude, yielding a discrete Representation Autoencoder (dRAE) with a fully utilized codebook and scalable budget.

Result: dRAE achieves high-fidelity reconstruction while preserving semantic integrity; codebooks scale to 131,072 entries with 100% utilization; training pipeline is simplified; strong performance across both understanding and generation tasks.

Conclusion: Addressing metric mismatch through angular, magnitude-decoupled quantization yields scalable, semantically coherent discrete representations, enabling effective integration with language models and robust performance on understanding and generation tasks.

Abstract: In this work, we aim to discretize the high-dimensional visual representations to bridge the gap with language models - a non-trivial challenge, as existing quantization methods suffer from codebook collapse, failing to scale while preserving semantic coherence. We identify the root cause as metric mismatch: standard Euclidean codebook objectives are fundamentally misaligned with the anisotropic geometry of representation space, leading to codebook embeddings with high-variance magnitude scales and uneven angular distributions that hinder scalability. To address this, we propose Hyper-Spherical Quantization (HSQ), which decouples semantic content from feature magnitude via angular routing, preventing code assignment from being dominated by scale rather than meaning. The resulting discrete Representation Autoencoder (dRAE) achieves high-fidelity reconstruction while preserving semantic integrity and supporting scalable codebook budget. Extensive experiments demonstrate consistent performance gains as the vocabulary size scales to 131{,}072, along with 100\% codebook utilization, simplified training pipeline, and strong performance across understanding and generation tasks.

</details>


### [36] [Bowel Obstruction Detection and Localization on Abdominal CT with Deep Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22173)
*Moritz Vandenhirtz,Andrea Agostini,Dana Belde,Mélanie Roschewitz,Ismaiel Chikh Bakri,Tilo Niemann,André Euler,Julia E Vogt*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Multi-task deep learning for automated bowel obstruction detection and transition zone localization on CT, with an interpretable, slice-level transition-point locator. Evaluated on 1,427 abdominal CTs, achieving 93% detection accuracy and 95% Hit@10 localization. First method to reliably localize the transition zone.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Alleviate increasing radiology workload by automatically detecting bowel obstruction and identifying the critical transition zone, enabling faster and more accurate clinical decision-making.

Method: A deep learning framework with a multi-task objective that jointly detects obstruction and localizes its transition zone. It includes an interpretable component that locates the suspected transition point within a slice via a probabilistic selection mask that constrains the classifier to a small image region.

Result: On an internal dataset of 1,427 abdominal CTs, the model achieved 93% test accuracy for obstruction detection and 95% Hit@10 for transition-zone localization; the approach is claimed to be the first to reliably localize the transition zone.

Conclusion: Demonstrates feasibility of automatic obstruction detection with reliable local localization of the transition zone, representing a significant advance toward automated identification of a key clinical landmark and potential to support radiologists in workflow.

Abstract: Bowel obstruction is a common and potentially life-threatening gastrointestinal condition. In the face of rising diagnostic workloads, the automated diagnosis of bowel obstruction on CT scans supports radiologists by accelerating detection and improving patient outcomes. In this work, we propose a deep learning framework with a multi-task objective that jointly detects bowel obstruction and localizes its transition zone. Additionally, we extend the method with an inherently interpretable classification method that locates the suspected transition point within a slice. It does so by learning a probabilistic selection mask that faithfully bases the classifier's prediction solely on a small image region. The proposed method is evaluated on an internal dataset comprising 1,427 abdominal CTs. Here, the model achieves an obstruction detection test accuracy of 93% and a Hit@10 transition zone localization of 95%. As the first method to reliably localize the transition zone, this marks a significant step towards the automated identification of this critical clinical landmark.

</details>


### [37] [LayoutLite: Token-Level Implicit Layout Analysis for Efficient Document OCR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22200)
*Xudong Liu,Bicheng Wan,Yulin Jin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LayoutLite is a lightweight plug-and-play module that compresses OCR visual tokens via token-level implicit layout analysis, yielding substantial speedups with minimal accuracy loss.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: End-to-end VLM-based document OCR is bottlenecked by大量 visual tokens (many irrelevant/blank margins). Explicit layout detection is costly and error-prone; a lightweight, annotation-free approach is desirable.

Method: Aggregate multi-layer visual features, score each visual token with a lightweight predictor to determine importance, and drop low-information tokens before the language decoder while preserving token positions. Train without annotations by casting token selection as reinforcement learning with a group-relative policy optimization objective driven by OCR output consistency and an auxiliary layout supervision signal.

Result: On OmniDocBench, LayoutLite substantially reduces visual token length and inference cost with negligible OCR accuracy loss. When integrated with FireRed-OCR and Logics-Parsing-V2 (up to 50% token compression), it maintains similar recognition scores while reducing prefill latency, FLOPs, and KV cache memory by over 40%, with only modest additional inference overhead.

Conclusion: Token-level implicit layout analysis is an effective, practical method to accelerate VLM-based OCR without explicit layout detection or heavy supervision.

Abstract: End-to-end OCR systems based on vision-language models have achieved strong performance in complex document OCR, but their efficiency is limited by the large number of visual tokens produced from document images. Many of these tokens correspond to blank margins or visually redundant regions, yet directly applying generic visual token compression methods may remove OCR-critical fine-grained details. In this paper, we propose LayoutLite, a lightweight plug-and-play module for efficient document OCR. Instead of relying on explicit document layout detection, LayoutLite performs implicit layout analysis at the token level between the vision encoder and the language decoder. It aggregates multi-layer visual representations from the vision encoder, and predicts an importance score for each visual token with a lightweight scoring network. Low-information tokens are then removed before entering the language decoder while preserving the original spatial positional information of retained tokens. To train LayoutLite without human annotations, we cast token selection as a reinforcement learning problem and optimize it with a group-relative policy optimization objective driven by OCR output consistency, together with an auxiliary layout supervision signal to stabilize training. Experiments on OmniDocBench demonstrate that LayoutLite can substantially reduce visual token length and inference cost with negligible degradation in recognition quality. We further evaluate LayoutLite on two OCR-specialized VLMs, FireRed-OCR and Logics-Parsing-V2. Under up to 50% token compression, LayoutLite preserves almost the same score on both models while reducing prefill latency, FLOPs, and KV cache memory by over 40%, with only a small additional inference overhead. These results show that token-level implicit layout analysis is an effective and practical approach for accelerating VLM-based OCR systems.

</details>


### [38] [Filling Before Advancing: Capability-Gap-Driven Post-Training for Scenario-Specialized Remote Sensing MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22205)
*Yuheng Zong,Minghua Wang,Xin Zhao,Zhi-Hui Zhan,Antonio Plaza,Jon Atli Benediktsson*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A capability-gap-driven post-training framework, Filling Before Advancing (FBA), fills prerequisite capability gaps before target-domain specialization for RS-MLLMs. It is instantiated on coastal harbor understanding with CPRS (three-layer supervision) and HarborEval (eight-track benchmark). FBA yields improvements over direct SFT and other baselines under similar budgets, and releases datasets, code, and weights.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: RS-MLLMs require fine-grained scenario specialization, but high-quality, scenario-specific data is scarce and capability coverage is incomplete. A sequential, gap-filling adaptation can improve efficiency and performance over vanilla direct fine-tuning.

Method: FBA comprises three ordered stages: (1) RS semantic anchoring for overhead-view visual-language alignment; (2) domain-bridge convergence for shared RS priors across target and bridging scenarios using multiple modalities; (3) evidence-grounded scenario tuning for downstream tasks. It introduces CPRS (Coastal-Port Remote Sensing), a three-layer supervision dataset, and HarborEval, an eight-track diagnostic benchmark. Comparisons are made against Direct-SFT and Collapsed-SFT under equivalent training budgets on LLaVA-v1.5 and Qwen3-VL.

Result: HarborEval scores improve from 57.95 (Direct-SFT) to 70.29 (FBA) on LLaVA-v1.5, and from 81.09 to 83.37 on Qwen3-VL. FBA outperforms Collapsed-SFT and leads on harbor-related VRSBench/RSVQA subsets and OpenEval. Stage-wise analyses validate progressive gap filling and the role of each stage.

Conclusion: Progressive, stage-wise gap filling via FBA yields consistent gains over direct or collapsed fine-tuning, supporting the premise that addressing prerequisite capabilities before scenario specialization can improve RS-MLLM adaptation. Public release of CPRS, HarborEval, code, and weights is provided.

Abstract: Remote sensing multimodal large language models (RS-MLLMs) have improved general aerial-image understanding. However, Earth observation applications require fine-grained scenario specialization, constrained by scarce high-quality scenario data and incomplete capability coverage. We formulate this adaptation as a capability-gap-driven post-training problem and propose filling before advancing (FBA). Rather than relying on single-stage supervised fine-tuning (SFT) over target-domain samples, FBA first fills prerequisite capability gaps before advancing toward scenario specialization. We instantiate FBA for coastal harbor understanding, a representative multi-source scenario, by constructing CPRS (Coastal-Port Remote Sensing), a three-layer supervision dataset coupled with three ordered stages: (1) RS semantic anchoring for overhead-view visual-language alignment; (2) domain-bridge convergence for shared RS priors across target and bridging scenarios under different modalities; and (3) evidence-grounded scenario tuning for downstream performance. We construct HarborEval, an eight-track diagnostic benchmark covering perception, spatial understanding, robustness, and generation. Under comparable training budgets, HarborEval increases from 57.95 with Direct-SFT to 70.29 with FBA on LLaVA-v1.5, and from 81.09 to 83.37 on Qwen3-VL. FBA also outperforms Collapsed-SFT and leads on harbor-related VRSBench/RSVQA subsets and OpenEval. Stage-wise and role-replacement analyses validate progressive gap filling and stage-specific roles. Public examples and release updates for CPRS, HarborEval, code, and trained weights are available at https://github.com/Z0ngL1ng/filling-before-advancing.

</details>


### [39] [Deep Convolutional Large-Margin $\ell_p$-SVDD for Visual Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22212)
*Alireza Dastmalchi Saei,Shervin Rahimzadeh Arashloo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A deep large-margin novelty-detection framework (DLM-SVDD) jointly learns CNN features and an explicit kernel-based boundary via ell_p-SVDD, with alternating Frank–Wolfe dual updates and margin-violation CNN updates; explores scalable kernel approximations and demonstrates improved performance under data imbalance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Tackle visual anomaly detection where anomalous samples are scarce and class distributions are highly imbalanced by combining adaptive feature learning with a principled, margin-aware decision boundary.

Method: Joint optimization of CNN representations and an explicit kernel boundary using the large-margin ell_p-SVDD objective. Alternates between Frank–Wolfe-based updates of the convex dual boundary and CNN updates guided by a smooth margin-violation loss. Studies kernel approximation strategies to improve scalability.

Result: Empirical evaluation on multiple standard benchmarks shows consistent improvements over baselines and competitive performance against state-of-the-art methods, with robustness under severe imbalance.

Conclusion: The method successfully merges representation learning with margin-aware, kernel-based boundary learning, offering a scalable framework for deep anomaly detection that benefits from explicit margins and optimized decision regions.

Abstract: Visual anomaly detection requires adaptive representations and reliable decision boundaries, particularly when anomalous training samples are scarce and class distributions are highly imbalanced. Classical kernel-based methods yield principled geometric decision regions but typically operate on fixed features, while deep detectors learn task-specific representations but often fail to provide an explicit margin-aware kernel boundary. In this study, we propose DLM-SVDD, a deep large-margin novelty-detection framework that jointly learns convolutional features and an explicit kernel-based decision boundary. By drawing on the large-margin $\ell_p$-Support Vector Data Description ($\ell_p$-SVDD) approach, the proposed method performs explicit margin maximization and nonlinear slack penalization while adapting the representation to the target task. To train the proposed model, we present an optimization scheme that alternates between a Frank--Wolfe--based update of the convex dual boundary and a CNN update step operating on a smooth margin-violation loss induced by the recovered boundary. To improve scalability, we analyze the efficiency--accuracy trade-offs for different kernel approximation strategies, deriving practical propositions for large-scale anomaly detection.
  Extensive experiments on multiple standard benchmarks show consistent performance improvements over the baseline and strong overall performance compared with state-of-the-art methods while illustrating that the proposed joint representation--boundary learning scheme remains effective under severe imbalanced class distributions.

</details>


### [40] [TRaM-VSR: Importance-Aware Token Routing and Merging for One-Step Diffusion Video Super-Resolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22231)
*Sicheng Gao,Zhuyun Zhou,Yixuan Liu,Tong Shen,Zongwei Wu,Radu Timofte*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TRaM-VSR introduces adaptive token routing for diffusion-transformer-based video super-resolution, combining motion cues and semantic similarity to prioritize tokens. An offline planner guides routing across network blocks, enabling high-fidelity local streams for critical tokens and compact global streams for others. This yields significant speedups with state-of-the-art quality and temporal consistency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Large-scale diffusion-transformer priors for VSR achieve high perceptual quality but incur quadratic complexity from dense spatio-temporal tokens. Existing efficiency methods risk detail loss and temporal flicker, especially in one-step diffusion. There is a need for adaptive, content-aware token management that preserves quality while reducing computation.

Method: Estimate token importance by fusing motion-sensitive temporal cues with semantic text similarity to identify dynamic objects and structural boundaries. Calibrate this importance with an offline planner to guide routing across optimally grouped network blocks. In each routed group, structurally critical tokens are processed in a high-fidelity local stream, while less informative tokens are aggregated into a compact global stream, both modulated by network depth and aligned with the multigranular nature of diffusion models.

Result: Extensive experiments show substantial inference acceleration while preserving state-of-the-art reconstruction quality and robust temporal consistency in VSR.

Conclusion: TRaM-VSR demonstrates that token routing and merging, driven by content- and network-level priors, can achieve efficient, high-quality VSR with improved temporal stability, offering a practical approach for diffusion-based VSR.

Abstract: Video super-resolution (VSR) using large-scale Diffusion Transformer (DiT) priors achieves exceptional perceptual quality but is often impractical due to the quadratic computational cost of processing dense spatio-temporal token sequences. Existing efficiency-oriented methods risk irreversible detail loss and temporal flickering, a vulnerability especially pronounced in one-step diffusion models. To address this, we propose TRaM-VSR, a Token Routing and Merging framework for adaptive token allocation, leveraging both context-aware video priors and network-level priors. First, token importance is estimated by fusing motion-sensitive temporal cues with semantic text similarity, isolating dynamic objects and structural boundaries. Next, this importance is further calibrated and adjusted by an offline planner to guide routing across optimally grouped network blocks. Technically, within each routed group, structurally critical tokens are processed in a high-fidelity local stream, while less informative tokens are aggregated into a compact global stream, both modulated by network depth and aligned with the multigranular nature of diffusion models. Extensive experiments show that TRaM-VSR accelerates inference significantly while preserving state-of-the-art reconstruction quality and robust temporal consistency. The code is available at https://github.com/Ree1s/TRaM-VSR.

</details>


### [41] [AgentHOI: Multi-Agent Reasoning for Human-Object-Interaction Video Generation via Implicit Representation Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22241)
*Ziyao Huang,Shunkai Li,Juan Cao,Chenyu Li,Youliang Zhang,Zixiang Zhou,Cong Wang,Yuan Zhou,Qinglin Lu,Fan Tang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes AgentHOI, a text-driven HOI video generator that uses multi-agent thinking and implicit text-to-motion alignment to generate interactive videos without explicit motion inputs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address the need for fine-grained, scalable HOI video generation that generalizes across objects and interactions beyond explicit motion control.

Method: A thinking-before-generation framework with multi-agent reasoning over perception, interaction, and motion planning. Builds interaction plans to guide diffusion-based video synthesis and introduces an implicit text-motion alignment strategy that distills text-to-motion priors into the video diffusion model, enabling HOI synthesis without explicit motion inputs at inference.

Result: Significant improvements in interaction naturalness, object appearance preservation, and adherence to complex textual instructions across object-centric tasks such as wearing and riding.

Conclusion: Bridges high-level textual intent and physical execution, enabling robust HOI synthesis without explicit motion inputs; public code release for reproducibility.

Abstract: Recent advances in video diffusion models have spurred interest in human-object interaction (HOI) video generation, which demands fine-grained control over interaction logic beyond single-subject animation. However, existing HOI methods rely heavily on explicit motion control, limiting scalability and generalization across diverse objects and interactions. In this study, we propose AgentHOI, a text-driven HOI video generation following a thinking-before-generation framework that bridges the gap between high-level textual intent and physical execution through multi-agent reasoning over perception, interaction, and motion planning. Building upon the generated interaction plans, we further strengthen text-driven motion understanding. We introduce an implicit text-motion alignment strategy that distills text-to-motion priors into the video diffusion model, enabling robust HOI synthesis without explicit motion inputs at inference. Experiments show that AgentHOI significantly improves interaction naturalness, object appearance preservation, and adherence to complex textual instructions across challenging object-centric scenarios such as wearing and riding. The code is available at https://github.com/bone-11/agenthoi.

</details>


### [42] [From level set evolution to threshold optimization: A grayscale level set framework for image segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22255)
*Xingkai Li,Jiebao Sun,Fanghui Song,Zhichang Guo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes a fast grayscale level-set segmentation framework for degraded images by showing length regularization is unnecessary under certain smoothness; reduces PDE evolution to 1D threshold search and demonstrates speed and performance on noisy and intensity-inhomogeneous images.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Segmentation of heavily degraded images with level-set methods is hindered by the length regularization term, which causes instability and high computational cost. A faster, robust approach under smoothness constraints is desired.

Method: The authors theoretically analyze the impact of the length term on the level-set gradient magnitude, show that under certain smoothness conditions the length term is nonessential, define a class of smooth images, construct grayscale level sets, and derive a framework that converts PDE evolution into a one-dimensional threshold search for fast segmentation on degraded images.

Result: The framework achieves significant computational speed improvements, particularly on large-scale images, while maintaining effective segmentation on degraded images. Experiments validate performance on heavily noisy and intensity-inhomogeneous images.

Conclusion: Length regularization is not essential under specified smoothness constraints; PDE evolution can be replaced with 1D threshold search to yield a fast, scalable segmentation framework for degraded images with competitive accuracy.

Abstract: The segmentation of multiple degradations has been a challenging problem in the field of image segmentation. Existing level set approaches commonly adopt a length regularization term to constrain the geometric shape of the segmentation contour. However, the introduction of the length term often results in numerical instability and high computational cost. In this paper, we show that the length term is not essential under certain smoothness constraints, and theoretically prove that the presence of the length term affects the property of $|\nabla φ|=1$. Based on the finding, we define a class of smooth images, construct the grayscale level set, and propose a fast segmentation framework for degraded images, such as heavily noisy images and intensity inhomogeneous images. The framework transforms PDE evolution into one-dimensional threshold search, which has significant advantages in computational speed, especially on large-scale images. Experiments validate the segmentation performance of the proposed framework on various degraded images.

</details>


### [43] [RadSight: Towards Perceptually Reliable Multimodal Radiology Image Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22293)
*Jianqin Liu,Weiwei Cao,Wanxing Chang,Ruifeng Yuan,Bowen Shi,Zhilin Zheng,Xianjie Zhang,Ling Zhang,Peng Wang,Jianpeng Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RadSight is a perception-driven medical MLLM that emphasizes low-level visual perception to achieve reliable clinical understanding; evaluated on Perception-Bench (1.13M samples, six tasks across 2D/3D radiology), it outperforms existing MLLMs, with strong gains in spatial grounding and diagnosis; trained on 8.37M perception-oriented data with progressive curriculum; code/models to be released.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current medical MLLMs frequently misinterpret visuals, due to a bottleneck in low-level visual perception. There's a need to trace and address failures from clinical tasks down to perception, and to ground outputs in native visual evidence.

Method: RadSight uses a dual 2D/3D encoder preserving imaging spatial structure, and a four-stage process: visual-language alignment, fine-grained visual perception, clinical diagnosis, diagnostic interpretation; trained on a large perception-oriented corpus with progressive curriculum learning.

Result: RadSight consistently outperforms baselines on Perception-Bench across six dimensions; particularly better at spatial grounding and clinical diagnosis; shows improvements on public 2D/3D benchmarks.

Conclusion: Robust low-level visual perception is a critical foundation for reliable clinical understanding; future work/code will be released publicly.

Abstract: Medical multimodal large language models (MLLMs) are increasingly expected to perform complex image understanding tasks, yet their reliability is often compromised by frequent errors in visual interpretation. To systematically trace these failures, we traverse the hierarchy from high-level clinical tasks down to fundamental visual perception. We therefore introduce Perception-Bench, a large-scale benchmark comprising 1.13 million samples that assesses medical MLLMs across six dimensions: attribute judgment, spatial grounding, spatial understanding, disease prediction, anomaly detection, and report generation, spanning both 2D and 3D radiology images. Our analysis on Perception-Bench reveals that existing MLLMs lack the ability to capture even the most basic lesion attributes, such as location, size, and density. This inability to ground clinical outputs in primary visual evidence reveals that the models' diagnostic unreliability is rooted in a critical but overlooked bottleneck in low-level visual perception. Motivated by this, we propose RadSight, a perception-driven MLLM built upon a dual 2D/3D encoder architecture that preserves native imaging spatial structures. RadSight formulates medical image understanding as a four-stage progressive process: visual-language alignment, fine-grained visual perception, clinical diagnosis, and diagnostic interpretation. The model is trained on an 8.37 million perception-oriented corpus using progressive curriculum learning. On Perception-Bench, RadSight consistently outperforms existing MLLMs across all six evaluation dimensions, with particularly strong gains in spatial grounding and clinical diagnosis. It also achieves consistent improvements on public 2D and 3D medical benchmarks, further demonstrating that robust low-level visual perception is a critical foundation for reliable clinical understanding. Code and model will be publicly available.

</details>


### [44] [fMRI2Face: A Full-HD fMRI-Video Dataset and Geometry-Guided Neural Decoding Framework for Dynamic Human Face Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22302)
*Jingyang Huo,Xiangru Huang,Chentao Shen,Yikai Wang,Yun Wang,Jianxiong Gao,Shihao Jin,Yanwei Fu,Jianfeng Feng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces fMRI-Face: a high-resolution fMRI–video dataset (62,856 samples) paired with controllable full-HD facial videos, and fMRI2Face, a geometry-guided neural video decoding framework that uses brain-derived appearance context and morphable 3D facial control via diffusion-based video generation to reconstruct dynamic faces from brain activity. Demonstrates improved fidelity, identity preservation, geometry, and motion over baselines, establishing a new benchmark for fMRI-based dynamic face reconstruction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address the lack of high-resolution, controllable neural datasets and the difficulty of recovering both identity-specific appearance and time-varying facial dynamics from fMRI. Provide a controlled platform to study dynamic face perception and enable neural decoding of complex social signals.

Method: 1) Data collection: record fMRI while participants watch controllable, photorealistic, full-HD facial videos (1920x1080), with controlled identity, expression, and head pose. 62,856 paired samples. 2) fMRI2Face: a two-branch control scheme: Brain-derived Appearance Context captures global identity-associated attributes; Morphable 3D Facial Control provides geometry-aware guidance for pose, expression, and non-rigid dynamics. Fusion via Neural-Controlled Video Diffusion with auxiliary latent completion to reconstruct high-fidelity videos directly from fMRI signals.

Result: fMRI2Face consistently improves reconstruction fidelity, preserves identity-related appearance, enhances facial geometry accuracy, and improves motion consistency compared to representative neural decoding baselines.

Conclusion: The combination of a controlled fMRI–video dataset and a geometry-guided diffusion-based decoding framework establishes a new benchmark platform for dynamic face reconstruction from brain activity and facilitates systematic studies of dynamic face perception.

Abstract: Reconstructing dynamic human faces from brain activity provides a powerful way to study how the mind perceives identity, expression, and facial motion. However, progress in fMRI-based face decoding has been limited by scarce controlled, high-resolution neural datasets and by methods that struggle to recover both identity-specific appearance and time-varying facial dynamics. We present fMRI-Face, the first fMRI dataset paired with controllable full-HD digital human facial videos rendered at 1920$\times$1080 resolution. During scanning, participants watched photorealistic, background-free facial videos with controlled identity, expression, and head pose, while fMRI activity was recorded. The resulting dataset contains 62,856 paired fMRI-video samples, providing a structured resource for studying dynamic face perception and reconstruction. Building on this dataset, we propose fMRI2Face, a geometry-guided neural video decoding framework for reconstructing facial videos from fMRI signals. fMRI2Face derives two complementary neural controls from brain activity: Brain-derived Appearance Context, which captures global identity-related visual attributes, and Morphable 3D Facial Control, which provides explicit geometry-aware guidance for pose, expression, and non-rigid facial dynamics. These controls are integrated through Neural-Controlled Video Diffusion with auxiliary latent completion, enabling high-fidelity facial video reconstruction directly from brain activity. Experiments show that fMRI2Face consistently improves reconstruction fidelity, identity preservation, facial geometry, and motion consistency over representative neural decoding baselines. Together, fMRI-Face and fMRI2Face establish a controlled platform for studying dynamic face perception and provide a new benchmark for fMRI-based digital human reconstruction.

</details>


### [45] [SLIP: Segmentation with Low-latency Interactive Prompting for 3D Medical Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22332)
*Baptiste Podvin,Alexandre Ancel,Flavio Milana,Chiara Innocenzi,Davide Arrigo,Federico Espinola Schulze,Guido Torzilli,Jacques Marescaux,Daniel George,Alexandre Hostettler,Toby Collins*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SLIP is an end-to-end trainable framework for interactive 3D medical image segmentation that separates image encoding from prompt-driven refinement using a patch memory bank, enabling low-latency, reversible prompting and stateful propagation across patches, achieving SOTA across 13 datasets with a controlled user study.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address limitations of patch-based interactive segmentation (high latency, poor responsiveness to successive prompts, no reversible prompting) and the lack of real-user evaluation; need an end-to-end trainable, generalizable solution.

Method: Propose SLIP: decouples image encoding from interactive reasoning; computes features once; uses a lightweight patch memory bank to store state shared across patches; updates predictions by propagating prompt context; supports reversible prompting; compatible with various encoders; single model trained across modalities; includes a controlled prospective user study with expert participants.

Result: SLIP attains state-of-the-art performance on 13 public datasets; lowers interaction latency; improves responsiveness; supports reversible prompting; higher user preference vs nnInteractive in a controlled study.

Conclusion: Separating image representation from interactive reasoning yields a flexible, efficient, and user-friendly interactive segmentation framework with broad encoder compatibility and strong generalization.

Abstract: Interactive deep image segmentation enables efficient medical image annotation by iteratively refining predictions from user prompts, such as positive and negative clicks. Recent patch-based methods, including nnInteractive, achieve strong segmentation performance but remain limited in annotation workflows by high interaction latency, limited responsiveness to successive interactions, and the lack of support for reversible prompting. Furthermore, evaluation relies predominantly on simulated rather than controlled real-user interaction studies. We present SLIP, an end-to-end trainable framework for interactive 3D medical image segmentation that decouples image encoding from prompt-guided refinement. Image features are computed once and reused, while a lightweight patch memory bank maintains an interaction-aware segmentation state shared across patches. This representation enables prediction updates by propagating interaction context throughout the image, supports reversible prompting without recomputing image features, and substantially reduces interaction latency. By separating image representation from interactive reasoning, SLIP remains compatible with a wide range of image encoders. We train a single SLIP model for general interactive segmentation across diverse anatomical structures and imaging modalities. Beyond standard simulated evaluation, we conduct a controlled prospective user study comparing manual segmentation, nnInteractive, and SLIP across three clinical annotation tasks, six expert participants, and subjective usability measures, addressing the limited human validation of interactive segmentation methods. SLIP achieves SOTA interactive segmentation performance across 13 public datasets while providing lower interaction latency, greater responsiveness, support for reversible prompting, and higher user preference than existing approaches.

</details>


### [46] [Time-Reversed Imaging: A Multimodal Benchmark and Framework for Reconstructing Past Human-Environment Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22352)
*Jorge Bacca,Kebin Contreras,Luis Toscano-Palomino,Mauro Dalla Mura*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Time-reversed imaging: infer past events from fading traces using multi-spectral signals. Introduces TRACE-HEI, a tri-modal dataset, and a multimodal inference pipeline that constrains a diffusion model with textual traces to reconstruct plausible past frames; demonstrates feasibility and sets a foundational direction for non-instantaneous scene understanding.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address the ill-posed problem of recovering past human–environment interactions from residual traces that fade over time, leveraging complementary modalities to reduce ambiguity and enable reasoning about past events.

Method: Create TRACE-HEI—the first tri-modal, synchronized dataset of actions (sitting, touching, moving objects, spills) captured across thermal, UV, and visible spectra up to three minutes post-contact. Develop a multimodal inference pipeline that (i) detects traces, (ii) extracts structured textual descriptions of the traces, and (iii) guides a vision-language diffusion model to reconstruct plausible past frames.

Result: Empirical evaluation shows that reconstructing recent past events from fading traces is challenging but feasible when modalities are combined to reduce ambiguity. TRACE-HEI provides a benchmark and demonstrates the viability of the proposed approach.

Conclusion: This work establishes the first computational and experimental foundation for time-reversed imaging, bridging vision, physics, and generative reasoning, and opening new avenues for scene understanding beyond instantaneous observation.

Abstract: We introduce time-reversed imaging, a new paradigm that infers what just happened in a scene from fading multimodal traces. Instead of extrapolating or interpolating video frames, our goal is to infer past human-environment interactions from residual physical imprints observable in thermal, ultraviolet, and visible spectra. To study this problem, we present TRACE-HEI, the first proof-of-concept dataset for time-reversed imaging, containing synchronized tri-modal video sequences of actions such as sitting, touching, moving objects, and liquid spills, captured across diverse materials and recorded up to three minutes after contact. To establish the benchmark, we propose a multimodal inference approach that extracts structured textual descriptions of detected traces and uses them to constrain a vision-language-guided diffusion model for reconstructing plausible past frames. Experiments show that inferring recent events from fading traces is challenging but feasible when complementary modalities reduce solution ambiguity. This work defines the first computational and experimental foundation for time-reversed imaging, bridging vision, physics, and generative reasoning, and opening new directions for scene understanding beyond instantaneous observation.

</details>


### [47] [SiPhy: Single-Image Physical Property Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22355)
*Hoang Le,Joonwoo Kwon,Elkhan Ismayilzada,Yufei Zhang,Zijun Cui*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SiPhy presents a single-image framework that predicts physical properties by aligning 3D cues with language-based material knowledge, using pseudo-voxel sampling, CLIP features, and a VLM-grounded material set, plus part-based and heaviness-aware refinements; it achieves state-of-the-art single-image performance across multiple benchmarks and shows potential as a data annotation engine for physical understanding from single-view imagery.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Inferring mass, stiffness, and elasticity from a single RGB image is valuable for simulation and embodied AI, but existing methods rely on multi-view data or strong supervision. A unified single-image approach that grounds visual cues to material knowledge is needed.

Method: From one RGB image, SiPhy samples pseudo-voxel points, extracts CLIP features, and grounds them to materials proposed by a vision-language model (VLM). A part-based contrastive aggregator enforces region consistency, and a heaviness-aware refinement improves thickness and volume estimation for dense objects.

Result: On ABO-500, MVImgNet-100, and PhysXNet-100, SiPhy achieves state-of-the-art single-image performance, surpassing multi-view reconstruction methods (e.g., MnRE improvement up to 93% over PUGS; density MAE reduced by 35.5% versus NeRF2Physics; Young's modulus error reduced by 23.5%). Real hand-object interaction datasets further validate its potential as a data-annotation engine for single-view physical understanding.

Conclusion: SiPhy demonstrates the viability of single-image physical-property reasoning by grounding 3D cues to language-based material knowledge and refining through region-consistent and heaviness-aware mechanisms, offering a strong single-image alternative to multi-view approaches and a promising tool for data annotation and simulation pipelines.

Abstract: Inferring physical properties such as mass, stiffness, and elasticity from a single image is essential for simulation and embodied AI, yet most existing approaches rely on multi-view reconstruction or physics-based supervision. We introduce SiPhy, a unified framework for single-image physical property reasoning that aligns 3D-aware visual cues, depth with language-based material knowledge. From one RGB image, SiPhy samples pseudo-voxel points, extracts CLIP features, and grounds them to material candidates proposed by a VLM. A part-based contrastive aggregator enforces region consistency, while a heaviness-aware refinement improves thickness and volume estimation for dense objects. Across ABO-500, MVImgNet-100, and PhysXNet-100, SiPhy achieves state-of-the-art single-image performance, surpassing multi-view reconstruction methods by improving mass MnRE by up to 93% (vs. PUGS), reducing density MAE by 35.5% (vs. NeRF2Physics), and lowering Young's modulus error by 23.5%. We further validate SiPhy on real hand-object interaction datasets, demonstrating its potential as a data annotation engine for physical understanding from single-view imagery.

</details>


### [48] [Active few-shot segmentation by reinforcing data selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22371)
*Chenlan Zhao,Benny Wong,Timothy F. Lundberg,Ahmed M. Elsayed,Abdallah Aljarkas,Hamad A. Aljamaan,Lynn Karam,Qianye Yang,Yipeng Hu,Claire C. Villette,Shaheer U. Saeed*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes a reinforcement learning framework to jointly optimize support-set selection for few-shot medical image segmentation, improving adaptation by exploiting complementarity among samples; validated on cross-institutional pelvic MRI with gains over baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Few-shot segmentation must adapt to new tasks with minimal labels; existing active selection methods score samples independently and neglect interactions, potentially missing complementary information important for adaptation.

Method: An RL agent selects a support set from an unlabeled pool to maximize downstream segmentation performance, optimizing the set jointly rather than scoring samples individually.

Result: The approach outperforms random selection and state-of-the-art methods on a cross-institutional pelvic MRI dataset, underscoring the benefit of support-set complementarity for adaptation.

Conclusion: Jointly optimizing support sets via reinforcement learning is effective for boosting few-shot medical image segmentation and highlights the value of considering sample interactions in data selection.

Abstract: Few-shot learning enables medical image segmentation models to adapt to new tasks using only a small number of labelled examples. However, adaptation performance depends strongly on which examples are selected for the support set. Effective support sets should capture relevant variation within the target domain and be informative for adaptation, with constituent samples providing complementary information. Despite this, existing active data selection approaches largely prioritise samples individually and do not explicitly account for interactions between examples. In this work, we propose a reinforcement learning framework for support-set selection in few-shot medical image segmentation, enabling support sets to be optimised jointly rather than through independent sample scoring. Given a pool of unlabelled candidate images, an agent directly predicts a support set that maximises downstream segmentation performance. Experiments on a cross-institutional pelvic MRI dataset demonstrate improvements over random selection and current state-of-the-art methods. Our findings highlight the importance of support-set complementarity for effective adaptation and demonstrate the potential of reinforcement learning for optimising adaptation sets.

</details>


### [49] [IR275K: A Benchmark for Infrared Multi-Frame Super-Resolution Toward Efficient Remote Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22380)
*Jie Deng,Heyang Wang,Changxin Wang,Junkai Shen,Hongyi Chen,Zhiping He,Hongxing Qi,Xudong Zhang,Jianyu Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: IR275K is a new infrared MFSR benchmark with 594 video sequences (275k frames) and a reproducible X4 protocol, plus an initial architectural probe (CGMamba) showing strong PSNR gains with low cost, and ablations highlighting the importance of 2D RoPE and spatial anchoring in SSM-based cross-frame fusion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address fragmented infrared MFSR evaluation across private datasets and ad-hoc protocols; capture infrared-specific challenges such as thermal contrast, sensor noise, weak texture, and platform-induced frame-to-frame variation; provide a reproducible benchmark and evaluation protocol to enable fair accuracy–efficiency comparisons.

Method: IR275K: curated infrared video benchmark with sequence-level train/val/test splits and an X4 evaluation protocol. Architectural probe CGMamba: a lightweight state-space model (10.90M params, 112.14 GFLOPs) using 2D rotary position encoding (2D RoPE) and center-guided cross-Mamba (CGCM) fusion for implicit multi-frame reconstruction.

Result: CGMamba achieves 33.19 dB PSNR, outperforming infrared single-image SR references by 0.35–0.52 dB at much lower computational cost. Ablation: removing 2D RoPE causes a 1.53 dB drop and grid-like artifacts, showing explicit spatial anchoring stabilizes cross-frame gating under infrared conditions.

Conclusion: IR275K provides a reproducible foundation for evaluating infrared MFSR methods; CGMamba offers a concrete starting point for spatially aware state-space model design in resource-constrained infrared sensing.

Abstract: Efficient processing is becoming increasingly important in infrared remote sensing, where satellite constellations produce large volumes of observations under constrained detector resolution, power, and downlink bandwidth. Multi-frame super-resolution (MFSR) offers a software-based route to spatial enhancement, but its evaluation in infrared sensing remains fragmented across private datasets and ad-hoc protocols. Existing benchmarks do not explicitly capture the thermal contrast, sensor noise, weak texture, and platform-induced frame-to-frame variation that characterize infrared video. We introduce IR275K, a curated benchmark containing 594 infrared video sequences and 275,196 frames. It provides sequence-level train/validation/test splits and a reproducible X4 evaluation protocol. As an initial architectural probe, we further evaluate CGMamba, a lightweight state-space model with 10.90M parameters and 112.14G FLOPs. CGMamba combines 2D rotary position encoding (2D~RoPE) with center-guided cross-Mamba (CGCM) fusion for implicit multi-frame reconstruction. It achieves 33.19dB PSNR, outperforming infrared single-image super-resolution references by 0.35--0.52~dB at substantially lower computational cost. Ablation results show that removing 2D~RoPE from CGCM causes a 1.53dB drop and severe grid-like artifacts. This indicates that explicit spatial anchoring is critical for stabilizing SSM-based cross-frame gating under infrared conditions. IR275K provides a reproducible foundation for accuracy--efficiency evaluation of infrared MFSR methods, while the architectural analysis offers a concrete starting point for spatially aware SSM design under resource-constrained infrared sensing. Dataset and evaluation resources are available at: https://github.com/InfraRecon7/IR275K.

</details>


### [50] [Correlation-Aware and Gaussianity-Preserving Robust Latent Angular Watermarking for Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22386)
*Yebin Zheng,Haonan An,Guang Hua,Zhiping Lin,Yuguang Fang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Latent Angular Watermarking (LAW) encodes watermark bits as antipodal angular relations in latent vectors of diffusion models, preserving Gaussianity and yielding a sparse, analyzable correlation structure; LAW-M further improves robustness by anchoring bits to stable latent dimensions; the approach includes a theoretical treatment of induced autocorrelation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current latent-domain watermarking struggles with violating latent Gaussianity, susceptibility to inversion/perturbation attacks, and breakdown of the i.i.d. latent assumption, which can degrade generation fidelity. There is also a lack of rigorous internal correlation analysis beyond external metrics like FID.

Method: Encode watermark bits as antipodal angles (±π/2) between disjoint pairs of latent elements, leveraging the rotation-invariant property of isotropic Gaussian to preserve Gaussianity. The decoding error variance scales as var(Δφ) ∝ 1/ρ^2 with latent pair norm ρ. Introduce LAW-M, which selects geometrically stable latent dimensions for carrying bits. Provide closed-form analysis of the induced latent autocorrelation, showing correlations occupy a sparse, structured off-diagonal pattern with fixed ±π/4 values.

Result: The work presents a theoretical framework for law-watering latent-domain watermarking: a rigorous characterization of the induced correlation degradation, closed-form autocorrelation of the watermarked latent, and a sparse, predictable correlation structure. It primarily offers analytical guarantees and design principles for robust watermark embedding.

Conclusion: LAW offers a non-intrusive watermarking scheme that preserves Gaussian latent structure and provides a principled, sparse correlation footprint, with LAW-M delivering further robustness by focusing on stable latent dimensions. Theoretical treatment of correlation degradation supports understanding of the latent impact and potential defense against attacks.

Abstract: Latent domain watermarking for diffusion models embeds watermarks directly into the latent prior, enjoying non-intrusiveness to model parameters and seamless integration with the generation process. However, due to the violation of latent Gaussianity or sensitivity to normal and malicious perturbations during latent inversion, existing methods are prone to watermark detection or removal attacks.
